I know it’s probably not really dangerous, but it’s gotten bad enough that I can’t really do some of my cleaning like I’m supposed to.\n\nDoctor: When you think about seeing a rat at work, do you ever feel like you're going to have a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I should call it a panic attack exactly, but it feels pretty close. If I hear scratching near a wall or see something move by a pipe, my body just reacts right away—my heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I feel like I have to get out before I can even think straight. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about rats when you're not at work or in a situation where they might be present?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t think I sit around thinking about them all the time or anything. It’s more when something reminds me, like if I’m going into a place where they could be. At home, I try not to think about it, I guess. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your fear of rats is excessive or unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know it’s probably more than it should be. It’s not like every old building or trash room is actually dangerous, and I understand that. But just the thought of seeing a rat gets me so worked up that I avoid parts of my cleaning job now. So… I guess it does feel unreasonable, even if I’m embarrassed to admit that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically of rats, or are you also afraid of other animals?\n\nPatient: It’s really rats, mostly. I mean, I don’t love seeing pests in general, but it’s the thought of a rat that gets to me so much that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore. Like in old buildings or trash rooms, even when I know it’s probably not truly dangerous… I still just can’t handle it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats happen in situations outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… not as much as at work, I guess. It’s mostly when I’m somewhere that reminds me of work, like an old building or near trash rooms, places like that. The thought of seeing one is what really gets me, even if I know it’s probably not actually dangerous. I feel silly saying that, but that’s how it is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know it’s probably not really dangerous, but it’s gotten bad enough that I can’t really do some of my cleaning like I’m supposed to.\n\nDoctor: When you think about seeing a rat at work, do you ever feel like you're going to have a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I should call it a panic attack exactly, but it feels pretty close. If I hear scratching near a wall or see something move by a pipe, my body just reacts right away—my heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I feel like I have to get out before I can even think straight. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about rats when you're not at work or in a situation where they might be present?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t think I sit around thinking about them all the time or anything. It’s more when something reminds me, like if I’m going into a place where they could be. At home, I try not to think about it, I guess. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your fear of rats is excessive or unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know it’s probably more than it should be. It’s not like every old building or trash room is actually dangerous, and I understand that. But just the thought of seeing a rat gets me so worked up that I avoid parts of my cleaning job now. So… I guess it does feel unreasonable, even if I’m embarrassed to admit that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically of rats, or are you also afraid of other animals?\n\nPatient: It’s really rats, mostly. I mean, I don’t love seeing pests in general, but it’s the thought of a rat that gets to me so much that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore. Like in old buildings or trash rooms, even when I know it’s probably not truly dangerous… I still just can’t handle it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats happen in situations outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… not as much as at work, I guess. It’s mostly when I’m somewhere that reminds me of work, like an old building or near trash rooms, places like that. The thought of seeing one is what really gets me, even if I know it’s probably not actually dangerous. I feel silly saying that, but that’s how it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats more about the animal itself or about what might happen if you were to encounter one?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the rat itself… or even just the thought of seeing one. I’m not really sitting there thinking something terrible is definitely going to happen, because I know an old building or a trash room isn’t automatically dangerous. But my reaction just takes over, and it’s gotten to where I can’t do certain parts of my cleaning job. I know that probably sounds a bit much, but that’s honestly what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats triggered by just seeing a rat, or is it also triggered by being in places where rats might be, even if you don't see one?\n\nPatient: It’s both, I think… not only if I actually see one. If I hear scratching near a wall, or something moves by a pipe, my body just reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I feel like I need to get out before I can really think it through. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it happens really fast.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by things that remind you of rats, but aren't actually rats, such as pictures or stories about rats?\n\nPatient: Um… sometimes, yes, but it’s more like if something makes me think one is there. Like if I hear scratching near a wall, or see something move by a pipe, my whole body just reacts before I can really stop it. My heart starts pounding and my hands shake, and I feel like I have to get out. I know it’s probably not reasonable… but that’s what happens.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know it’s probably not really dangerous, but it’s gotten bad enough that I can’t really do some of my cleaning like I’m supposed to.\n\nDoctor: When you think about seeing a rat at work, do you ever feel like you're going to have a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I should call it a panic attack exactly, but it feels pretty close. My heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I feel like I need to get out before I can really think it through. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats happen in situations outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… not as much as at work, I guess. It’s mostly when I’m somewhere that reminds me of work, like an old building or near trash rooms, places like that. The thought of seeing one is what really gets me, even if I know it’s probably not actually dangerous. I feel silly saying that, but that’s how it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats more about the animal itself or about what might happen if you were to encounter one?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the rat itself… or even just the thought of seeing one. I’m not really sitting there thinking something terrible is definitely going to happen, because I know an old building or a trash room isn’t automatically dangerous. But my reaction just takes over, and it’s gotten to where I can’t do certain parts of my cleaning job. I know that probably sounds a bit much, but that’s honestly what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats triggered by just seeing a rat, or is it also triggered by being in places where rats might be, even if you don't see one?\n\nPatient: It’s both, I think… not only if I actually see one. If I hear scratching near a wall, or something moves by a pipe, my body just reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I feel like I need to get out before I can really think it through. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it happens really fast.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by things that remind you of rats, but aren't actually rats, such as pictures or stories about rats?\n\nPatient: Um… sometimes, yes, but it’s more like if something makes me think one is there. Like if I hear scratching near a wall, or see something move by a pipe, my whole body just reacts before I can really stop it. My heart starts pounding and my hands shake, and I feel like I have to get out. I know it’s probably not reasonable… but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by just thinking about being in a situation where rats might be present, even if you're not actually thinking about seeing a rat at that moment?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Like, even if I’m not picturing an actual rat, just knowing I might have to go into an old building area or a trash room can start that feeling. I tell myself it’s probably not dangerous, and I know it’s just part of the job, but the thought of maybe running into one has gotten bad enough that I avoid parts of my cleaning work. I know that may sound silly, but it’s been hard to get past.\n\nDoctor: Are you also afraid of mice or other rodents, or is your fear specifically limited to rats?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s mainly rats… that’s what I’m here about. The thought of seeing a rat has gotten so bad that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore, like old building areas or trash rooms. I know those places probably aren’t really dangerous, and I’m trying not to make it sound worse than it is, but rats are the thing that really sets me off.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything about rats in particular that triggers your fear, or would you react similarly to other similar-sized animals?\n\nPatient: I think it’s really rats in particular. I mean, I don’t want to sound dramatic, but the thought of seeing one has gotten bad enough that I’m avoiding parts of my cleaning work now… like old building areas or trash rooms. Even when I know it’s probably not actually dangerous, it still gets to me. Other animals don’t seem to do that to me in the same way.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by thinking about rats in a context other than work, such as at home or in a park?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes up around work, I think. Like when I know I might have to go into an old building area or a trash room, even if I know it’s probably not truly dangerous. The thought of seeing a rat there has gotten bad enough that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore. I’m not sure I notice it as much in places like home or a park, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats ever occur when you are not thinking about work or being in a work-related environment?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, I don’t think. I mean, the main thing is the thought of seeing a rat when I’m at work, like in an old building area or a trash room. It’s gotten bad enough that I can’t do some of those parts of my cleaning job anymore, even though I know it’s probably not actually dangerous. I hope that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats ever interfere with your daily life or activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really… I mean, outside of work I haven’t noticed it getting in the way much. It’s mostly tied to those work areas where I might run into one. If there’s something specific you mean by daily life, I can try to think about it more.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. The thought of seeing a rat there has gotten bad enough that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore. I’m not sure I notice it as much in places like home or a park, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats ever occur when you are not thinking about work or being in a work-related environment?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, I don’t think. It’s gotten bad enough that I can’t do some of those parts of my cleaning job anymore, even though I know it’s probably not actually dangerous. I hope that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats ever interfere with your daily life or activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really… I mean, outside of work I haven’t noticed it getting in the way much. It’s mostly tied to those work areas where I might run into one. If there’s something specific you mean by daily life, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of rats affected your relationships with friends or family in any way?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really affected my family or friends. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… well, I’m a little embarrassed to say it, but it’s gotten hard for me at work. I clean buildings, and lately just the thought that I might see a rat—like in an old building or near the trash room—makes me avoid parts of the job. I know it’s probably not really dangerous, but it’s gotten bad enough that I can’t really do some of my cleaning like I’m supposed to.\n\nDoctor: When you think about seeing a rat at work, do you ever feel like you're going to have a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I should call it a panic attack exactly, but it feels pretty close. If I hear scratching near a wall or see something move by a pipe, my body just reacts right away—my heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I feel like I have to get out before I can even think straight. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about rats when you're not at work or in a situation where they might be present?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t think I sit around thinking about them all the time or anything. It’s more when something reminds me, like if I’m going into a place where they could be. At home, I try not to think about it, I guess. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your fear of rats is excessive or unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know it’s probably more than it should be. It’s not like every old building or trash room is actually dangerous, and I understand that. But just the thought of seeing a rat gets me so worked up that I avoid parts of my cleaning job now. So… I guess it does feel unreasonable, even if I’m embarrassed to admit that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically of rats, or are you also afraid of other animals?\n\nPatient: It’s really rats, mostly. I mean, I don’t love seeing pests in general, but it’s the thought of a rat that gets to me so much that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore. Like in old buildings or trash rooms, even when I know it’s probably not truly dangerous… I still just can’t handle it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats happen in situations outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… not as much as at work, I guess. It’s mostly when I’m somewhere that reminds me of work, like an old building or near trash rooms, places like that. The thought of seeing one is what really gets me, even if I know it’s probably not actually dangerous. I feel silly saying that, but that’s how it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats more about the animal itself or about what might happen if you were to encounter one?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the rat itself… or even just the thought of seeing one. I’m not really sitting there thinking something terrible is definitely going to happen, because I know an old building or a trash room isn’t automatically dangerous. But my reaction just takes over, and it’s gotten to where I can’t do certain parts of my cleaning job. I know that probably sounds a bit much, but that’s honestly what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats triggered by just seeing a rat, or is it also triggered by being in places where rats might be, even if you don't see one?\n\nPatient: It’s both, I think… not only if I actually see one. If I hear scratching near a wall, or something moves by a pipe, my body just reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I feel like I need to get out before I can really think it through. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it happens really fast.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by things that remind you of rats, but aren't actually rats, such as pictures or stories about rats?\n\nPatient: Um… sometimes, yes, but it’s more like if something makes me think one is there. Like if I hear scratching near a wall, or see something move by a pipe, my whole body just reacts before I can really stop it. My heart starts pounding and my hands shake, and I feel like I have to get out. I know it’s probably not reasonable… but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by just thinking about being in a situation where rats might be present, even if you're not actually thinking about seeing a rat at that moment?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Like, even if I’m not picturing an actual rat, just knowing I might have to go into an old building area or a trash room can start that feeling. I tell myself it’s probably not dangerous, and I know it’s just part of the job, but the thought of maybe running into one has gotten bad enough that I avoid parts of my cleaning work. I know that may sound silly, but it’s been hard to get past.\n\nDoctor: Are you also afraid of mice or other rodents, or is your fear specifically limited to rats?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s mainly rats… that’s what I’m here about. The thought of seeing a rat has gotten so bad that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore, like old building areas or trash rooms. I know those places probably aren’t really dangerous, and I’m trying not to make it sound worse than it is, but rats are the thing that really sets me off.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything about rats in particular that triggers your fear, or would you react similarly to other similar-sized animals?\n\nPatient: I think it’s really rats in particular. Even when I know it’s probably not actually dangerous, it still gets to me. I mean, the main thing is the thought of seeing a rat when I’m at work, like in an old building area or a trash room. It’s gotten bad enough that I can’t do some of those parts of my cleaning job anymore, even though I know it’s probably not actually dangerous. Is that what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Well… I guess there are some places I try not to deal with if I can help it. Like basements, storage rooms, laundry areas, or those alley dumpsters… if there’s another way or someone else can do it, I’ll usually turn that down. 

If I absolutely have to clean somewhere like that, I keep the door open and just try to get through it fast. I’m pretty scared the whole time, but I do it if I have to. Is that the kind of thing you mean?

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.228, F40.248\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… well, I’m a little embarrassed to say it, but it’s gotten hard for me at work. I clean buildings, and lately just the thought that I might see a rat—like in an old building or near the trash room—makes me avoid parts of the job. I know it’s probably not really dangerous, but it’s gotten bad enough that I can’t really do some of my cleaning like I’m supposed to.\n\nDoctor: When you think about seeing a rat at work, do you ever feel like you're going to have a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I should call it a panic attack exactly, but it feels pretty close. If I hear scratching near a wall or see something move by a pipe, my body just reacts right away—my heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I feel like I have to get out before I can even think straight. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about rats when you're not at work or in a situation where they might be present?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t think I sit around thinking about them all the time or anything. It’s more when something reminds me, like if I’m going into a place where they could be. At home, I try not to think about it, I guess. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your fear of rats is excessive or unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know it’s probably more than it should be. It’s not like every old building or trash room is actually dangerous, and I understand that. But just the thought of seeing a rat gets me so worked up that I avoid parts of my cleaning job now. So… I guess it does feel unreasonable, even if I’m embarrassed to admit that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically of rats, or are you also afraid of other animals?\n\nPatient: It’s really rats, mostly. I mean, I don’t love seeing pests in general, but it’s the thought of a rat that gets to me so much that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore. Like in old buildings or trash rooms, even when I know it’s probably not truly dangerous… I still just can’t handle it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats happen in situations outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… not as much as at work, I guess. It’s mostly when I’m somewhere that reminds me of work, like an old building or near trash rooms, places like that. The thought of seeing one is what really gets me, even if I know it’s probably not actually dangerous. I feel silly saying that, but that’s how it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats more about the animal itself or about what might happen if you were to encounter one?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the rat itself… or even just the thought of seeing one. I’m not really sitting there thinking something terrible is definitely going to happen, because I know an old building or a trash room isn’t automatically dangerous. But my reaction just takes over, and it’s gotten to where I can’t do certain parts of my cleaning job. I know that probably sounds a bit much, but that’s honestly what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats triggered by just seeing a rat, or is it also triggered by being in places where rats might be, even if you don't see one?\n\nPatient: It’s both, I think… not only if I actually see one. If I hear scratching near a wall, or something moves by a pipe, my body just reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I feel like I need to get out before I can really think it through. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it happens really fast.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by things that remind you of rats, but aren't actually rats, such as pictures or stories about rats?\n\nPatient: Um… sometimes, yes, but it’s more like if something makes me think one is there. Like if I hear scratching near a wall, or see something move by a pipe, my whole body just reacts before I can really stop it. My heart starts pounding and my hands shake, and I feel like I have to get out. I know it’s probably not reasonable… but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by just thinking about being in a situation where rats might be present, even if you're not actually thinking about seeing a rat at that moment?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Like, even if I’m not picturing an actual rat, just knowing I might have to go into an old building area or a trash room can start that feeling. I tell myself it’s probably not dangerous, and I know it’s just part of the job, but the thought of maybe running into one has gotten bad enough that I avoid parts of my cleaning work. I know that may sound silly, but it’s been hard to get past.\n\nDoctor: Are you also afraid of mice or other rodents, or is your fear specifically limited to rats?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s mainly rats… that’s what I’m here about. The thought of seeing a rat has gotten so bad that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore, like old building areas or trash rooms. I know those places probably aren’t really dangerous, and I’m trying not to make it sound worse than it is, but rats are the thing that really sets me off.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything about rats in particular that triggers your fear, or would you react similarly to other similar-sized animals?\n\nPatient: I think it’s really rats in particular. I mean, I don’t want to sound dramatic, but the thought of seeing one has gotten bad enough that I’m avoiding parts of my cleaning work now… like old building areas or trash rooms. Even when I know it’s probably not actually dangerous, it still gets to me. Other animals don’t seem to do that to me in the same way.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by thinking about rats in a context other than work, such as at home or in a park?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes up around work, I think. Like when I know I might have to go into an old building area or a trash room, even if I know it’s probably not truly dangerous. The thought of seeing a rat there has gotten bad enough that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore. I’m not sure I notice it as much in places like home or a park, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats ever occur when you are not thinking about work or being in a work-related environment?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, I don’t think. I mean, the main thing is the thought of seeing a rat when I’m at work, like in an old building area or a trash room. It’s gotten bad enough that I can’t do some of those parts of my cleaning job anymore, even though I know it’s probably not actually dangerous. I hope that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats ever interfere with your daily life or activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really… I mean, outside of work I haven’t noticed it getting in the way much. It’s mostly tied to those work areas where I might run into one. If there’s something specific you mean by daily life, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of rats affected your relationships with friends or family in any way?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really affected my family or friends. I mean, nobody’s complained or anything. It’s mostly just… my own thing at work. At home or with people, I don’t really notice it causing problems. Does that sound right?\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats ever cause you significant distress or impairment in your personal life, outside of your job?\n\nPatient: Honestly, outside of my job, I don’t think it causes me major problems. I mean, I might not like the idea of rats anywhere, of course, but I haven’t noticed it really interfering with my personal life. It’s mainly the work stuff. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is there anything outside of work that you completely avoid because you're afraid of rats?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess there are some places I try not to deal with if I can help it. Like basements, storage rooms, laundry areas, or those alley dumpsters… if there’s another way or someone else can do it, I’ll usually turn that down. \n\nIf I absolutely have to clean somewhere like that, I keep the door open and just try to get through it fast. I’m pretty scared the whole time, but I do it if I have to. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… well, I’m a little embarrassed to say it, but it’s gotten hard for me at work. I clean buildings, and lately just the thought that I might see a rat—like in an old building or near the trash room—makes me avoid parts of the job. I know it’s probably not really dangerous, but it’s gotten bad enough that I can’t really do some of my cleaning like I’m supposed to.\n\nDoctor: When you think about seeing a rat at work, do you ever feel like you're going to have a panic attack?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I should call it a panic attack exactly, but it feels pretty close. If I hear scratching near a wall or see something move by a pipe, my body just reacts right away—my heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I feel like I have to get out before I can even think straight. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about rats when you're not at work or in a situation where they might be present?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I don’t think I sit around thinking about them all the time or anything. It’s more when something reminds me, like if I’m going into a place where they could be. At home, I try not to think about it, I guess. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your fear of rats is excessive or unreasonable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know it’s probably more than it should be. It’s not like every old building or trash room is actually dangerous, and I understand that. But just the thought of seeing a rat gets me so worked up that I avoid parts of my cleaning job now. So… I guess it does feel unreasonable, even if I’m embarrassed to admit that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear specifically of rats, or are you also afraid of other animals?\n\nPatient: It’s really rats, mostly. I mean, I don’t love seeing pests in general, but it’s the thought of a rat that gets to me so much that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore. Like in old buildings or trash rooms, even when I know it’s probably not truly dangerous… I still just can’t handle it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats happen in situations outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… not as much as at work, I guess. It’s mostly when I’m somewhere that reminds me of work, like an old building or near trash rooms, places like that. The thought of seeing one is what really gets me, even if I know it’s probably not actually dangerous. I feel silly saying that, but that’s how it is.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats more about the animal itself or about what might happen if you were to encounter one?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the rat itself… or even just the thought of seeing one. I’m not really sitting there thinking something terrible is definitely going to happen, because I know an old building or a trash room isn’t automatically dangerous. But my reaction just takes over, and it’s gotten to where I can’t do certain parts of my cleaning job. I know that probably sounds a bit much, but that’s honestly what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats triggered by just seeing a rat, or is it also triggered by being in places where rats might be, even if you don't see one?\n\nPatient: It’s both, I think… not only if I actually see one. If I hear scratching near a wall, or something moves by a pipe, my body just reacts right away. My heart starts pounding, my hands shake, and I feel like I need to get out before I can really think it through. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it happens really fast.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by things that remind you of rats, but aren't actually rats, such as pictures or stories about rats?\n\nPatient: Um… sometimes, yes, but it’s more like if something makes me think one is there. Like if I hear scratching near a wall, or see something move by a pipe, my whole body just reacts before I can really stop it. My heart starts pounding and my hands shake, and I feel like I have to get out. I know it’s probably not reasonable… but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by just thinking about being in a situation where rats might be present, even if you're not actually thinking about seeing a rat at that moment?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. Like, even if I’m not picturing an actual rat, just knowing I might have to go into an old building area or a trash room can start that feeling. I tell myself it’s probably not dangerous, and I know it’s just part of the job, but the thought of maybe running into one has gotten bad enough that I avoid parts of my cleaning work. I know that may sound silly, but it’s been hard to get past.\n\nDoctor: Are you also afraid of mice or other rodents, or is your fear specifically limited to rats?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s mainly rats… that’s what I’m here about. The thought of seeing a rat has gotten so bad that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore, like old building areas or trash rooms. I know those places probably aren’t really dangerous, and I’m trying not to make it sound worse than it is, but rats are the thing that really sets me off.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything about rats in particular that triggers your fear, or would you react similarly to other similar-sized animals?\n\nPatient: I think it’s really rats in particular. I mean, I don’t want to sound dramatic, but the thought of seeing one has gotten bad enough that I’m avoiding parts of my cleaning work now… like old building areas or trash rooms. Even when I know it’s probably not actually dangerous, it still gets to me. Other animals don’t seem to do that to me in the same way.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of rats ever triggered by thinking about rats in a context other than work, such as at home or in a park?\n\nPatient: Mostly it comes up around work, I think. Like when I know I might have to go into an old building area or a trash room, even if I know it’s probably not truly dangerous. The thought of seeing a rat there has gotten bad enough that I can’t do some parts of my cleaning job anymore. I’m not sure I notice it as much in places like home or a park, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats ever occur when you are not thinking about work or being in a work-related environment?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, I don’t think. I mean, the main thing is the thought of seeing a rat when I’m at work, like in an old building area or a trash room. It’s gotten bad enough that I can’t do some of those parts of my cleaning job anymore, even though I know it’s probably not actually dangerous. I hope that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Does your fear of rats ever interfere with your daily life or activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really… I mean, outside of work I haven’t noticed it getting in the way much. It’s mostly tied to those work areas where I might run into one. If there’s something specific you mean by daily life, I can try to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Has your fear of rats affected your relationships with friends or family in any way?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really affected my family or friends.
